Q1. Tens digit \(4\) and digit sum \(13\) are fixed. What is the number?
A. \(51\)
B. \(49\)
C. \(50\)
D. \(48\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
4+u=13\Rightarrow u=9
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 49.

\[
N=10\times 4+9=49
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 49 is 13.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(49\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=9
\]

\[
N=49
\]

Fast result: \(49\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---

Q3. The number \(732x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(144\) and \(306\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 144 is 144.

\[
18\times 8=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 306.

\[
306=18\times 17+0
\]

\[
306-0=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{306-144}{18}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{306}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{143}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-7=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(122\) and \(203\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 122 is 128.

\[
8\times 16=128
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 200 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 203.

\[
203=8\times 25+3
\]

\[
203-3=200
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{200-128}{8}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{203}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{121}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-15=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q6. Between \(238\) and \(355\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?

Explanation:

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 238 is 240.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 348 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 355.

\[
355=12\times 29+7
\]

\[
355-7=348
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{348-240}{12}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[
29-19=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q7. Two buses leave a stand every \(198\) and \(306\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(3029\)
B. \(3366\)
C. \(3703\)
D. \(4039\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
198=2\times 3^{2}\times 11
\]

\[
306=2\times 3^{2}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,306)=3366
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 3366.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3366\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(198,306)=\frac{198\times 306}{\operatorname{HCF}(198,306)}=3366
\]

Fast result: \(3366\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge
---
Q8. After prime factorising \(108\), what is the number of positive divisors?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(108)=(2+1)\times (3+1)=12
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (3+1)
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the number of odd divisors?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(2+1)
\]

\[
O=3
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(2+1)=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count

Q15. After prime factorising \(108\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(294\)
B. \(308\)
C. \(266\)
D. \(280\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(108)=280
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 280.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. After prime factorising \(24\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(364954\)
B. \(414720\)
C. \(497664\)
D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,84)=336
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 336.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(336\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,84)=\frac{48\times 84}{\operatorname{HCF}(48,84)}=336
\]

Fast result: \(336\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q25. Power \(3^{47}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
3\rightarrow 0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(47-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{47}\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(47-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[
3^{47}\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---

Q28. Power \(2^{32}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(32-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
2^{32}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(32-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

\[
2^{32}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_nested_remainder
---

\[

\]

\[
(85-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(85-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q41. Number \(64\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+4=64
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+6=46
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-4|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-4|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
10+2=12
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 50! = 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q52. Between \(184\) and \(346\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 184 is 192.

\[
12\times 16=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 346.

\[
346=12\times 28+10
\]

\[
346-10=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{336-192}{12}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{346}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{183}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-15=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q53. Two buses leave a stand every \(60\) and \(120\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(114\)
B. \(120\)
C. \(126\)
D. \(132\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
120=2^{3}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,120)=120
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(120\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,120)=\frac{60\times 120}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,120)}=120
\]

Fast result: \(120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q88. The number \(732x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q89. Two cyclic events repeat every \(156\) and \(84\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1201\)
B. \(1310\)
C. \(983\)
D. \(1092\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
156=2^{2}\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
84=2^{2}\times 3\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(156,84)=1092
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1092.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1092\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(156,84)=\frac{156\times 84}{\operatorname{HCF}(156,84)}=1092
\]

Fast result: \(1092\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q91. Power \(13^{87}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(87-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
13^{87}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(87-1)\bmod 10+1=7
\]

\[
2^{87}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q103. Between \(161\) and \(281\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 161 is 162.

\[
9\times 18=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 279 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 281.

\[
281=9\times 31+2
\]

\[
281-2=279
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{279-162}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{281}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{160}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-17=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q117. Between \(207\) and \(371\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 207 is 216.

\[
18\times 12=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 371.

\[
371=18\times 20+11
\]

\[
371-11=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{360-216}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{371}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{206}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-11=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q121. Power \(3^{80}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(80-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
3^{80}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(80-1)\bmod 6+1=2
\]

\[
3^{80}\equiv 2\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\]

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[

\]

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(943\).

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(35-1)\bmod 20+1=15
\]

\[
7^{35}\equiv 943\pmod{1000}
\]

Fast result: \(943\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
2^{29}\equiv 6\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Q149. Between \(240\) and \(358\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 240 is 240.

\[
12\times 20=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 348 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 358.

\[
358=12\times 29+10
\]

\[
358-10=348
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{348-240}{12}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{358}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{239}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-19=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q150. Two buses leave a stand every \(56\) and \(104\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(692\)
B. \(728\)
C. \(764\)
D. \(801\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
56=2^{3}\times 7
\]

\[
104=2^{3}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=728
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(728\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=\frac{56\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(56,104)}=728
\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(26\)
B. \(29\)
C. \(27\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+2=52
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+5=25
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-2|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-2|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q168. Between \(126\) and \(253\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 126 is 128.

\[
8\times 16=128
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 248 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 253.

\[
253=8\times 31+5
\]

\[
253-5=248
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{248-128}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{253}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{125}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-15=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[
\sigma(12)=28
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 28.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(28\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q172. When \(13^{95}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
4\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(95-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
13^{95}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(95-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
4^{95}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

\]

\[
N=216
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(216\).

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q184. The number \(57x92\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=23
\]

\[
S+x=23+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 122 is 126.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 272.

\[
272=9\times 30+2
\]

\[
272-2=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{270-126}{9}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{272}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{121}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=360
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 360.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(360\).

Shortcut

\]

Fast result: \(360\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q204. For exponent \(104\), what ending digit does \(8^{104}\) have?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
8\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(104-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
8^{104}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(104-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
8^{104}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Explanation:

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(99-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{99}\equiv 1\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(99-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
7^{99}\equiv 1\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q226. The number \(37x94\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 23; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

C. \(105\)
D. \(110\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[

\]

\[
105=3\times 5\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(105,105)=105
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 105.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(105\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(105,105)=\frac{105\times 105}{\operatorname{HCF}(105,105)}=105
\]

Fast result: \(105\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 77 is 14.

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=77
\]

Fast result: \(77\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
12+2=14
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 60! = 14.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{60}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{60}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q238. Between \(155\) and \(327\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 155 is 156.

\[
12\times 13=156
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 327.

\[
327=12\times 27+3
\]

\[
327-3=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{324-156}{12}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{327}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{154}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
27-12=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(945\)
Answer: C

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
300=2^{2}\times 3\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{1}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
300\times 3=900
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(900\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=3
\]

Fast result: \(900\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q249. The number \(13x58\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q250. For the number \(1440\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(5897\)
B. \(4423\)
C. \(4914\)
D. \(5405\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q252. When \(3^{86}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(86-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{86}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(86-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

\[
3^{86}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q261. Between \(185\) and \(320\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(22\)
B. \(25\)
C. \(23\)
D. \(24\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 185 is 186.

\[
6\times 31=186
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 318 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 320.

\[
320=6\times 53+2
\]

\[
320-2=318
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{318-186}{6}+1=22+1=23
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 23.
Hence, there are 23 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{320}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{184}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
53-30=23
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q287. Between \(239\) and \(340\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 239 is 243.

\[
9\times 27=243
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 333 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 340.

\[
340=9\times 37+7
\]

\[
340-7=333
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{333-243}{9}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{340}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{238}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
37-26=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+4=34
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 4+3=43
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-4|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-4|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q307. Between \(110\) and \(249\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 110 is 112.

\[
8\times 14=112
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 248 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 249.

\[
249=8\times 31+1
\]

\[
249-1=248
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{248-112}{8}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{249}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{109}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-13=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q308. Cycle position \(74\) is needed for powers of \(19\) modulo \(17\). What is the remainder?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(74-1)\bmod 8+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
19^{74}\equiv 4\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(74-1)\bmod 8+1=2
\]

\[
2^{74}\equiv 4\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

p=(42-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
8^{42}\equiv 4\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q333. Number \(65\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+5=65
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 5+6=56
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-5|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-5|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q336. Between \(123\) and \(266\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 123 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 266.

\[
266=18\times 14+14
\]

\[
266-14=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{252-126}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{266}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{122}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-6=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification

\[

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q356. The number \(992x6\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 26; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Answer: C

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q367. Between \(129\) and \(264\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 129 is 132.

\[
6\times 22=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 264.

\[
264=6\times 44+0
\]

\[
264-0=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{264-132}{6}+1=22+1=23
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 23.
Hence, there are 23 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{264}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{128}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
44-21=23
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+6=56
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+5=65
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-6|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-6|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

Cycle length = 2.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(60-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

Shortcut

\[

\[

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q392. The number \(9x341\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=9
\]

\[
9-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
9-x=0
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=9\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
9-9=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

\[

\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+4=64
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-6|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-6|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

\]

\[
N=27000
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27000\).

\[

Fast result: \(27000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q404. Between \(189\) and \(307\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 189 is 192.

\[
12\times 16=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 307.

\[
307=12\times 25+7
\]

\[
307-7=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{300-192}{12}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{307}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{188}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-15=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
q=\left\lceil\frac{628}{72}\right\rceil
\]

\[
N=72\times q=648
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 648.
\[
N=648
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(648\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=648
\]

Fast result: \(648\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q415. The number \(1x870\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Answer: D

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 91.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 19.

\[
19+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[

\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
19+x=24
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
19+5=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

D. \(643\)
Answer: C

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,153)=612
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 612.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(612\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,153)=\frac{36\times 153}{\operatorname{HCF}(36,153)}=612
\]

Fast result: \(612\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 12.

\[
17^{69}\equiv 12\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
4^{69}\equiv 12\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q444. Between \(237\) and \(357\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(20\)
B. \(21\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(22\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 237 is 240.

\[
6\times 40=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 354 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 357.

\[
357=6\times 59+3
\]

\[
357-3=354
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{354-240}{6}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{357}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{236}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
59-39=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q468. The number \(136x0\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 10; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
10+x=18
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
10+8=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,234)=1638
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1638\).

\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q470. When \(3^{54}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(54-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
3^{54}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(54-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{54}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q475. The number \(69x16\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=22
\]

\[
S+x=22+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q481. Between \(130\) and \(279\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 130 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 279.

\[
279=15\times 18+9
\]

\[
279-9=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-135}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{279}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{129}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-8=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=4
\]

\[
4-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
4-x=0
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
4-4=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(20\).

\[

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q518. The number \(42x07\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=9
\]

\[
9+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
9+x=11
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
9+2=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 37.

\[
N=10\times 3+7=37
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 37 is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(37\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=37
\]

Fast result: \(37\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q522. Cycle position \(80\) is needed for powers of \(19\) modulo \(17\). What is the remainder?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(80-1)\bmod 8+1=8
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
19^{80}\equiv 1\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(80-1)\bmod 8+1=8
\]

\[
2^{80}\equiv 1\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q525. The number \(158x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q528. Between \(202\) and \(379\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(20\)
B. \(21\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(22\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 202 is 207.

\[
9\times 23=207
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 378 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 379.

\[
379=9\times 42+1
\]

\[
379-1=378
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{378-207}{9}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{379}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{201}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
42-22=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\left\lfloor\frac{40}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{16}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{40}{32}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(38\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q549. The number \(592x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 22.

\[
22+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q550. Power \(3^{89}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(89-1)\bmod 5+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
3^{89}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(89-1)\bmod 5+1=4
\]

\[
3^{89}\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-8
\]

\[
-8+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-8+x=0
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-8+8=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(1122\)

\[

\]

\[
255=3\times 5\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,255)=1020
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1020.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1020\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,255)=\frac{60\times 255}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,255)}=1020
\]

Fast result: \(1020\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q569. Between \(125\) and \(253\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 125 is 126.

\[
9\times 14=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 253.

\[
253=9\times 28+1
\]

\[
253-1=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{252-126}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{253}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{124}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-13=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q575. Between \(173\) and \(279\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(14\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 173 is 176.

\[
8\times 22=176
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 272 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 279.

\[
279=8\times 34+7
\]

\[
279-7=272
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{272-176}{8}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{279}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{172}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-21=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q577. For exponent \(77\), what ending digit does \(12^{77}\) have?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
12\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(77-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
12^{77}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(77-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{77}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(3\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+6=66
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+6=66
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-6|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-6|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q583. The number \(392x6\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=20
\]

\[
S+x=20+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(109-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{109}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(109-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{109}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q604. Between \(114\) and \(254\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 114 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 254.

\[
254=18\times 14+2
\]

\[
254-2=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{252-126}{18}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{254}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{113}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-6=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q613. The number \(40x83\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-1
\]

\[
-1+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-1+x=0
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-1+1=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-9|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-9|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
3\rightarrow 0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(70-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
3^{70}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(70-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
3^{70}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q633. The number \(2x125\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=6
\]

\[
6-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
6-x=0
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
6-6=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q654. Between \(181\) and \(304\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 181 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 304.

\[
304=15\times 20+4
\]

\[
304-4=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-195}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{304}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{180}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-12=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q655. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q663. The number \(8x870\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 23; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q664. When \(7^{93}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(93-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
7^{93}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(93-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
2^{93}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q670. The number \(854x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
A=8
\]

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

\[
8-x=0
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification

\[
8-8=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q687. The number \(436x0\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=13
\]

\[
S+x=13+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q688. When \(11^{51}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(51-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
11^{51}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
2^{51}\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q690. Digits \(6\) and \(7\) form a two-digit number. What is the difference between it and its reversed number?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+7=67
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+6=76
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-7|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-7|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).
