Q697. The number \(7x094\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 116 is 120.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 223.

\[
223=12\times 18+7
\]

\[
223-7=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{216-120}{12}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
21+x=27
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
21+6=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q731. The number \(6x094\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+1=31
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+3=13
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-1|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-1|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q739. Between \(169\) and \(323\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(24\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(25\)
D. \(26\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 169 is 174.

\[
6\times 29=174
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 318 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 323.

\[
323=6\times 53+5
\]

\[
323-5=318
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{318-174}{6}+1=24+1=25
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 25.
Hence, there are 25 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(25\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{323}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{168}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
53-28=25
\]

Fast result: \(25\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q745. The number \(414x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 17.

\[
17+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
17+x=21
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
17+4=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

6\times 31=186
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 300.

\[
300=6\times 50+0
\]

\[
300-0=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{300-186}{6}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{300}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{181}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
50-30=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q763. The number \(870x4\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q764. When \(2^{91}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(91-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
2^{91}\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 7th term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(91-1)\bmod 12+1=7
\]

\[
2^{91}\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

Final Answer

\]

Q794. The number \(298x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 23.

\[
23+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q804. Between \(213\) and \(341\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(14\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(16\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 213 is 216.

\[
9\times 24=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 333 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 341.

\[
341=9\times 37+8
\]

\[
341-8=333
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{333-216}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{341}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{212}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
37-23=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=25
\]

\[
S+x=25+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
25+x=27
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
25+2=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q831. Between \(208\) and \(303\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 208 is 216.

\[
9\times 24=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 297 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 303.

\[
303=9\times 33+6
\]

\[
303-6=297
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{297-216}{9}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{303}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{207}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-23=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(36\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q839. Between \(146\) and \(280\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 146 is 162.

\[
18\times 9=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 280.

\[
280=18\times 15+10
\]

\[
280-10=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{270-162}{18}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{280}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{145}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-8=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q840. The power \(8^{31}\) is very large. What are its final digit?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
8\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(31-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
8^{31}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(31-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
8^{31}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q853. The number \(476x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 19.

\[
19+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
19+x=24
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
19+5=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q860. The number \(6x216\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q870. The number \(8x870\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=23
\]

\[
S+x=23+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q873. Between \(178\) and \(316\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 178 is 180.

\[
9\times 20=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 316.

\[
316=9\times 35+1
\]

\[
316-1=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{315-180}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{316}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{177}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-19=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q877. The number \(892x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 25.

\[
25+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
25+x=30
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
25+5=30
\]
\[
30\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q887. When \(3^{42}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(42-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
3^{42}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(42-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{42}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_remainder_pattern

Q896. Between \(212\) and \(339\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 212 is 216.

\[
8\times 27=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 339.

\[
339=8\times 42+3
\]

\[
339-3=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{336-216}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{339}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{211}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
42-26=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q898. Power \(2^{70}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(70-1)\bmod 10+1=10
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{70}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 10th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(70-1)\bmod 10+1=10
\]

\[
2^{70}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q904. The number \(39x14\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q911. The number \(176x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=18
\]

\[
18-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
18-x=11
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
18-7=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q921. Between \(144\) and \(253\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 144 is 144.

\[
9\times 16=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 253.

\[
253=9\times 28+1
\]

\[
253-1=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{252-144}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{253}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{143}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-15=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Hence, remainder = 13.

Q932. The number \(610x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 13.

\[
13+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
13+x=18
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
13+5=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q947. Between \(209\) and \(322\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 209 is 216.

\[
12\times 18=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 322.

\[
322=12\times 26+10
\]

\[
322-10=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{312-216}{12}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{322}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{208}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-17=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q948. Power \(13^{31}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(31-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
13^{31}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(31-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{31}\equiv 0\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q953. The number \(592x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[

\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
11+7=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q973. Power \(7^{63}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(63-1)\bmod 10+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
7^{63}\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(63-1)\bmod 10+1=3
\]

\[
7^{63}\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q977. The number \(776x2\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 22.

\[
22+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+2=52
\]

\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
52\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+2\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q985. Between \(221\) and \(371\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 221 is 224.

\[
8\times 28=224
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 368 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 371.

\[
371=8\times 46+3
\]

\[
371-3=368
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{368-224}{8}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{371}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{220}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
46-27=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 48.

\[
N=10\times 4+8=48
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 48 is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(48\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=48
\]

Fast result: \(48\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_digit_constraints
---
Q1000. Between \(144\) and \(305\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 144 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 305.

\[
305=15\times 20+5
\]

\[
305-5=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-150}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{305}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{143}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-9=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
